[hazards]
# How many places the translator rewrites that may change what a program means: methods moved into a class that then
# read another name than they did (a local declared between the record and the method, which the class cannot see or
# which hides an earlier one) or give other types than the field that declared them (43), and fields that a record is
# given `mut` on, declared or inherited, which an interface of another file declares without it (12). A ceiling that
# only falls: hoist the local above the record, or rename it, make the types agree, and give the interface the `mut`,
# in Teal.

[read]
# How many of the readers of the syntax tree ([`build.syntax_compared`]) answer a file otherwise than the fork's readers
# do of the same file. The stub of a `local function f(): never` has `error()` for its body, as the stub of the
# function of a record does; the fork leaves the body empty, since it does not mark a local function `never`. The
# stub is read by no one but the stdlib's own, which has no such function, so the port keeps the right body.
build/flow.tl 1

# A key is `<file> <code>`. A file with more errors of a code than pinned is refused, and one with fewer is told to
# lower its pin. A rewrite or a later piece only lowers pins. A change that adds or sharpens a rule of the checker may
# raise some, or add some for a new code, and says why in its message: the places the rule newly refuses, and the lines
# it adds to the files it edits. A file whose translation did not parse, and now does, is pinned at what it has: the
# errors the checker finds in a file it could not see are not a rule newly broken. The pins below are grouped by
# code, with the rule or the later piece that brings each to zero.
